   1|                                                                 Monty Tech School of Continuing Education
   2|                                                          The Monty Tech School of Continuing Education continues to update and add
   3|                                                          courses that emphasize a commitment to excellence by offering affordable,
   4|                                                          quality, and enjoyable educational experiences. For the fall of 2015, Monty Tech
   5|                                                          offered 67 classes with 667 registrations, and during the Spring 2016 semester,
   6|                                                          there were 66 postgraduate and continuing studies courses, with 609 registrations.
   7|                                                          The program, now under the leadership of Director Mary May-Lucchese, is
   8|                                                          looking forward to expanding the number of small business partnerships and
   9|                                                          increasing the number and quality of personal enrichment classes - sewing,
  10|                                                          knitting, acrylic painting and sculpting, to name a few. In addition, the Director
  11|                       has almost doubled the catalog distribution from 80,000 to 155,000 copies, and modified the saturation
  12|                        market to provide the popular evening programs with additional exposure.
  13|                       The School of Continuing Education is also looking forward to introducing a new software program with
  14|                       robust reporting features and a very intuitive student and instructor portal. This web-based software
  15|                       enhancement is critical to the program and very high on our list of priorities. The new online
  16|                       registration feature will, no doubt, be received positively by students and instructors alike.
  17|                        Spring 2017 will see our new ESL Beginner 1 and Beginner 2 courses come to fruition. These courses,
  18|                        coupled with our career and certificate classes, speak directly to course combinations that provide our
  19|                       community members an opportunity to grow, evolve and take their new skill set to the workforce.
  20|                       The success (and sustainability) of an adult education program is based on hiring quality instructors and
  21|                       meeting the needs of the community. We are in line to accomplish both! Our goal in the coming year
  22|                       includes increasing our adult education course offerings, with a concentration on career programs. Stay
  23|                       tuned for Pharmacy Technician, Medical Billing and Coding, Auto Damage Appraiser and
  24|                        Apprenticeship opportunities at the Monty Tech School of Continuing Education.
  25|                                                                               Practical Nursing Program
  26|                       The Practical Nursing Program is designed to prepare graduates to practice safely, ethically and in a
  27|                        caring manner for patients who are experiencing common variations in health status in diverse health care
  28|                       settings. This mission which is consistent with the philosophy and goals of the Montachusett Regional
  29|                       Vocational Technical School District accomplishes the following:
  30|                                 Identifies a strong relationship between academic and vocational preparation
  31|                              • Stresses the importance of developing critical thinking skills to function safely, effectively, and
  32|                                   productively in an ever-changing technical and diverse society
  33|                                   Supports the maintenance of a positive and caring learning and practice environment
  34|                       The above mission and philosophy were met by the Practical Nursing program by several different
  35|                        methods.
  36|                        On June 23, 2016, a graduating class of 32 students completed the Practical Nursing Program and entered
  37|                       the nursing profession. The class achieved an initial NCLEX pass rate of 91%, with 29 of the 32
  38|                       graduates achieving 100% pass rate for the NCLEX-PN exam. Three graduates are waiting to sit their
  39|                       NCLEX-PN exam. 94% of the 2016 graduates are currently employed in the health care profession
  40|                       throughout Massachusetts, and many are working within the eighteen cities and towns of the Monty Tech
  41|                        school district as LPNs in various health care settings, ranging from long term care, sub-acute care to
  42|                       mental health facilities.
  43|                                                                                                    135

===PAGE 136=== (ocr, 42 lines)
   1|                            The Monty Tech Practical Nursing Program continues to develop the "LPN to BSN Bridge" relationship
   2|                            with Fitchburg State University. Several 2016 graduates are pursuing seats in the LPN to BSN program at
   3|                            ESU and will be continuing their education to the Bachelors in Nursing. Many present class applicants cited
   4|                            as their attraction to Monty Tech as the opportunity to complete a ten-month accelerated program and then
   5|                            bridge to Fitchburg State University.
   6|                            Monty Tech Practical Nursing students are now completing patient scenarios in the Sim Lab on a weekly
   7|                            basis in Term 2 and Term 3. The Faculty Sim team has implemented National League of Nursing (NLN)
   8|                            patient scenarios consistent with our curriculum frameworks and have also utilized the Sim lab setting to
   9|                           instruct students in developing nursing skills that they may not be experiencing in the clinical setting. In-
  10|                            structors have successfully developed a pediatric patient scenario that is consistent with present student
  11|                            learning outcomes and the clinical pediatric setting.
  12|                            The Practical Nursing Program Faculty implemented new student learning outcomes across the curriculum
  13|                            adopted from the Massachusetts Board of Higher Education Nurse of the Future Initiative LN Competen-
  14|                            cies. The competencies have been recognized nationally as being consistent with current LN practice. The
  15|                           Faculty will assess the revised student learning outcomes and our content to assure consistency with the new
  16|                            NCLEX-PN Detailed Test Plan that will be released in March 2017.
  17|                            The class of 2016 performed a community service project adopting a Monty Tech family in need referred by
  18|                            Student Support Services. The nursing students purchased holiday gifts and food for the family, making sure
  19|                            they were consistent with the family's requests or needs.
  20|                            With an eye toward the program's future, forty-seven applicants were accepted to the Class of 2017, and
  21|                            forty-five students enrolled and will continue in the tradition of high-quality practical nursing preparation.
  22|                            Looking Ahead
  23|                            While the Montachusett Regional Vocational Technical School District educational community is certainly
  24|                            proud of the achievements of our talented students, faculty, and staff, we continue to have an eye toward the
  25|                            future, always committed to improving our vocational and academic programming, strengthening key part-
  26|                            nerships, and maintaining facilities that contribute to student success and achievement. As we look ahead,
  27|                           there are a number of programs and initiatives that we expect will have a positive impact on our school and
  28|                            students for years to come.
  29|                            Expand partnerships with area businesses: The Machine Technology program at Monty Tech has a long
  30|                            history of successfully placing students in machine and manufacturing shops in the area. Program instruc-
  31|                           tors work closely with a nineteen-member program advisory committee to ensure instruction and training
  32|                            equipment aligns with current industry standards, and welcome their guidance in this regard. With an eye
  33|                           toward expanding opportunities for students interested in pursuing occupations in this high-demand indus-
  34|                            try, program partner L. S. Starrett Company has suggested adding lessons in quality control and metrology
  35|                           to the existing curriculum. Program instructors anticipate reconfiguring the instructional space to bring in
  36|                            updated equipment, and will also add two industry-recognized certification exams to the curriculum. By
  37|                            standardizing metrology instruction and providing students with opportunities to earn additional credentials,
  38|                            program instructors are ensuring Monty Tech Machine Technology students are poised for a successful
  39|                            school-to-work transition upon graduation.
  40|                            Expand partnerships with area colleges and universities: Monty Tech students currently benefit from a
  41|                            number of articulation and dual enrollment agreements with surrounding post-secondary partners.
  42|                                                                                                    136

===PAGE 137=== (ocr, 27 lines)
   1|                          In the coming years, we hope to expand those opportunities in two vocational-technical areas: Information
   2|                          Technology and Early Childhood Education. By the end of 2016-2017, Monty Tech Information Technolo-
   3|                          gy instructors will submit an articulation request to MWCC officials, to determine how our curriculum may
   4|                          align with MWCC's Computer Information Systems Transfer Track (CIT) program. Additionally, instructors
   5|                          in Monty Tech's Early Childhood Education program will continue discussions with Fitchburg State Univer-
   6|                          sity officials, exploring the potential of embedding one college-level course into the senior year curriculum.
   7|                          School officials continue to discuss the merits of aligning curriculum, securing appropriate adjunct professor
   8|                          credentials, and costs associated with tuition and fees for the agreed upon course.
   9|                          Expand Access to Instructional Technology: A Monty Tech education is effective because efforts are made
  10|                          to continually assess our programming, instructional equipment, and our educational resources. Through
  11|                          this assessment, it has become clear that while so many students have access to technology away from the
  12|                          school, many do not. It is important, then, to arm every student with the tools necessary to succeed not only
  13|                          in the workplace, but in the classroom, as well. The district is pleased to announce a whole-school technolo-
  14|                          gy initiative, culminating in a September 2016 Chromebook distribution to each of our 1,435 students. It is
  15|                          our hope that these devices will support learning, increase and streamline communication with teachers, and
  16|                          promote collaboration among student peers.
  17|                          Expand vocational-technical educational opportunities: As you know, Montachusett Regional Vocational
  18|                          Technical School is home to twenty vocational-technical programs, and currently serves over 1,400 students
  19|                          from eighteen cities and towns in North Central Massachusetts. We are so proud of our students and accom-
  20|                          plishments, and every year we strive to provide only the best educational programs, both academic and voca-
  21|                          tional, to each and every student. As we review our student demographic, the regional workforce data, and
  22|                          feedback we have received from students interested in attending our school, we consider the benefits of add-
  23|                          ing new vocational programs. We are pleased to announce that Monty Tech is bringing our 21% program to
  24|                          the school - Animal/Veterinary Science. The new program, open to students Fall 2017, will respond to
  25|                          much-needed workforce training and community services, and will effectively prepare students to enter both
  26|                          college and career pathways upon graduation.

   3|                         The Annual Town Meeting was called to order at 9:05 A.M. in the Lunenburg High School Au-
   4|                         ditorium (it was noted that this is the last town meeting to be held in the Cushing Auditorium
   5|                         as the new Middle/High School will be completed soon) a quorum was declared present and the

   8|                         Moderator for the District) and would appoint Tom Bodkin to serve as Moderator for that ar-
   9|                         ticle.
  10|                         ARTICLE 1. To see if the Town will vote to re-authorize in accordance with MGL Chapter 44,
  11|                         §53E½, the Ambulance Billing Revolving Fund. The purpose of this fund will be to cover the cost
  12|                         associated with compensating third party advanced life support companies and third party billing
  13|                         companies that contract and supply services to the Town. Five percent (5%) of the total revenue gen-
  14|                         erated by ambulance services rendered and 100% of the revenue generated by Advanced Life Sup-
  15|                         port services rendered shall be deposited into said revolving fund. The proceeds of said Revolving
  16|                         Fund shall be expended by the Fire Department without further appropriation for the sole purpose of
  17|                         reimbursing the aforementioned companies for services rendered to the Town; expenditures from the
  18|                         revolving fund may not exceed $65,000 during the fiscal year without the approval of the Board of
  19|                         Selectmen and Finance Committee or take any other action relative thereto. (Submitted by Fire
  20|                         Chief) Board of Selectmen recommends approval. Finance Committee recommends approval. VOT-
  21|                         ED UNANIMOUS CONSENT

  26|                         not limited to appraisals and payment of accrued taxes; such funds may also be expended for im-

===PAGE 157=== (ocr, 36 lines)
   1|                                                                       Annual Town Meeting - May 5, 2016
   2|                          ARTICLE 39. To see if the Town will vote to amend the Zoning Bylaw, Section 6.0, General Pro-
   3|                          visions Affecting All Districts, by deleting Sections 6.1.2 Design of Off Street Parking and Loading
   4|                          Spaces and 6.4.3 Design Requirements and adding a new Section 6.4.3 Off Street Parking Design
   5|                          Standards, or take any action relative thereto. (Submitted by Planning Board) Board of Selectmen
   6|                          and Finance Committee will make recommendation at town meeting.
   7|                          6.4.3. Off-Street Parking Design Standards
   8|                          General Parking & Loading Provisions
   9|                                Location
  10|                                1. Required off-street parking spaces shall be provided on the same lot as, and loading bays
  11|                               shall be provided next to, the principal or accessory use they are required to serve.
  12|                               2. No area may be utilized and counted as both a required parking space and a required loading
  13|                               bay, without the approval of the Planning Board. Shared parking/loading areas shall only be
  14|                               used for loading prior to opening or after closing of all uses located on the site in question.
  15|                               However, maneuvering aisles and driveways may serve both required parking and loading bays
  16|                               if they meet the design standards of each. Existing areas used for both parking and loading shall
  17|                               be counted for loading purposes.
  18|                               3. Required off-street parking spaces or loading bays may be wholly or partly enclosed in a
  19|                               structure.
  20|                               4. Off-street parking spaces required for two or more buildings, uses, or establishments on a
  21|                               single lot may be provided in a common lot.
  22|                                Driveways
  23|                                1. Each parking space and loading bay shall be connected by a driveway to a street or to an inte-
  24|                                rior drive that leads to a street.
  25|                               2. The number of driveways permitting entrance to and exit from a lot shall be limited to two
  26|                               per street line. Driveways shall be located to minimize conflict with traffic on public streets and
  27|                               where good visibility and sight distances are available to observe approaching pedestrian and
  28|                               vehicular traffic.
  29|                               3. All curb cuts and access driveways shall be located in the Zoning District in which the site's
  30|                                primary use is permitted.
  31|                               4. The maximum grade of any outdoor driveway is 12%.
  32|                               5. No Driveway shall require that vehicles back out onto public or private ways.
  33|                                Setbacks
  34|                                     1. Each parking space or driveway serving a one-family or two-family dwelling shall be set
  35|                          back five feet from any side lot line and rear lot line, with the exception of common driveways
  36|                                                                                                    157

===PAGE 158=== (ocr, 32 lines)
   1|                                                                        Annual Town Meeting - May 5, 2016
   2|                           and shall be designated on a plan and is issued a building permit by the Building Commissioner.
   3|                                 2. No parking is permitted in the front yard other than in a designated parking space or drive-
   4|                                 way.
   5|                                 3. All paved parts of all parking spaces, driveways and maneuvering aisles shall be set back
   6|                                 from any wall of a principal building and from any lot line or zoning boundary line as indicated
   7|                                    District                Residential Dis-                  Street Line                 All Other Lot              Wall of Principal
   8|                                                                 trict Line                                                     Lines                      Building
   9|                             RA; RB; 0; R,                           N/A                            25                             5                             5
  10|                             VCD
  11|                             LB/R;                                   25                             25                            10                             5
  12|                            RC; C                                     40                            30                            15                             5
  13|                             OP/I                                     50                           30                                                            5
  14|                                 *all measurements are in feet
  15|                           Exceptions to the Table Are:
  16|                               a. One and Two-Family homes
  17|                                     b. A parking space located within a structure otherwise permitted in such area
  18|                                 4. No parking space, internal driveway or loading bay, whether required or otherwise provided,
  19|                                 shall be located, wholly or partly, within the right-of-way of a street.
  20|                                 5. All access to parking shall be by driveways meeting the requirements of this Section; curbs,
  21|                                 wheel stops, screening or similar barriers must be installed to prevent vehicles from being
  22|                                 parked or driven within required setback areas.
  23|                                  Screening for Parkins
  24|                                 1. In all residential and mixed uses, or on a lot in any district which abuts or is across the street
  25|                                 from a residential use, any outdoor parking lot, all loading bays, maneuvering aisles and drive-
  26|                                 ways shall be screened in a manner to protect abutting lots from the glare of headlights, noise
  27|                                 and other nuisance factors.
  28|                                 2. Any parking lot, which is a principal use, must be screened along driveways and around the
  29|                                 entire perimeter of the parking lot. The entrance to driveways, to the extent practicable, shall be
  30|                                 located on the side near nonresidential uses or on streets or highways leading to nonresidential
  31|                                 areas.
  32|                                                                                                    158

===PAGE 159=== (ocr, 33 lines)
   1|                                                                        Annual Town Meeting - May 5, 2016
   2|                                3. Screening shall consist of:
   3|                                      a. A strip of land at least four feet wide, densely planted with combination of shrubs, and
   4|                                      trees with or without earthen berms which shall have a combined height of at least four feet
   5|                                      high at the time of planting and which are of a type that may be expected to form a year-
   6|                                     round dense screen at least six feet high within three years;
   7|                                      b. A wall, barrier, or fence of uniform appearance at least five feet high above finished
   8|                                      grade. Such wall, barrier or fence may be opaque or perforated, provided that not more than
   9|                                      50% of the face is open; or
  10|                                      c. Any combination of a & b approved by the Planning Board through Site Plan Approval.
  11|                                4. Such screening shall be maintained in good condition at all times. Such screening or barrier
  12|                                may be interrupted by entrances or exits and shall have no signs attached thereto other than
  13|                                those permitted in the district.
  14|                                Design Standards
  15|                                Dimensions
  16|                                    a. Parking spaces and maneuvering aisles shall have the minimum dimensions set forth in the
  17|                                    following table and elsewhere in this section:
  18|                          Table 1
  19|                                    S = Standard Parking Space C= Compact Parking Space
  20|                                                           Width of                    Depth of                       Width of                   Unit Parking
  21|                                    Angle of                                                                       Maneuvering
  22|                                    Parking            Parking Space            Parking Space**                           Aisle                        DepthR
  23|                                                          S             C             S                             S               C              S              C
  24|                                  61° to 90°                          8.5*                           15            22              20             60             50
  25|                                   46° to 60°              9          8.5                            15                                           56             48
  26|                                   45°                     9          8.5                            15             14             13             53             47
  27|                                   Parallel               8             8                           18             12              12            n/a            n/a
  28|                                                                                                    NOTES:
  29|                                    * Where one or both of the long sides of a parking space abut a wall or similar obstruction,
  30|                                    the width shall be 12 feet.
  31|                                    ** Up to 2 feet of unpaved landscaped space may be included in the depth provided there are
  32|                                    no obstructions to the vehicles overhang.
  33|                                                                                                   159

===PAGE 160=== (ocr, 39 lines)
   1|                                                                          Annual Town Meeting - May 5, 2016
   2|                                     b. To count as a required parking space, a parallel parking space shall have maneuvering
   3|                                     space at least 20 feet deep in front of it in an aisle parallel to and abutting such parking space.
   4|                                     c. Where columns of a building or structure are located in a parking lot (such as a parking
   5|                                     garage under a building) no part of a column may be within three feet of a maneuvering aisle
   6|                                      or within the minimum dimensions of a parking space.
   7|                                      d. The width of a driveway for one-way use shall be a minimum of ten (10) feet and for two-
   8|                                      way use shall be a minimum of twenty (20) feet and a maximum of thirty (30) feet, as meas-
   9|                                     ured at the setback line.
  10|                                     e. Where access or egress is provided for a parking lot, or one or more loading bays, such ac-
  11|                                     cess or egress shall be so arranged to provide a circulation system or maneuvering space on
  12|                                     the lot so that all vehicles may exit from and enter onto a public street by being driven in a
  13|                                     forward direction and no vehicle shall be required to enter or leave by backing and no vehicle
  14|                                     shall have to stand within a street right-of-way waiting to enter the lot.
  15|                                 2. Number of compact car spaces. In parking lots containing more than 20 spaces, not more than
  16|                                 33% of such spaces may be designed for use by compact cars. Such compact car spaces shall be
  17|                                 located in one or more continuous areas and shall not be intermixed with spaces designed for
  18|                                 standard cars and shall be clearly designated by signs or pavement marking. In parking lots with
  19|                                 20 or fewer parking spaces, spaces designed for use by compact cars are not permitted.
  20|                                 3. Loading bays. All required loading bays must have minimum dimensions as follows: 30 feet
  21|                                 long, 12 feet wide and 14 feet high. Each loading bay shall have a maneuvering space equal to
  22|                                 its length. Where the long portion of a loading bay abuts a wall, column or other obstacle, or in
  23|                                 other cases where the permitting authority requests, evidence shall be provided that the loading
  24|                                 bay and its maneuvering space are adequate to accommodate large motor vehicles and trailers.
  25|                                 All required loading bays shall have an adequate travel isle to provide access. This may be sepa-
  26|                                 rately located or through the parking area. In either instance the "Truck Route" shall be clearly
  27|                                 delineated by signage or pavement marking. An adequate travel path shall provide width and
  28|                                 turning radii appropriate to a WB-55 or the largest truck expected to deliver goods to the site,
  29|                                 whichever is larger.
  30|                                 4. Marking. In a parking lot or loading area, the surface of the parking lot or loading area shall
  31|                                 be painted, marked or otherwise delineated so that the location of the parking spaces and load-
  32|                                 ing bays is apparent, and signs shall be erected indicating that loading bays, and, if necessary,
  33|                                 compact or other reserved parking spaces, are reserved for such use. Where 50% or more of the
  34|                                 required parking spaces in a parking lot are assigned, such as to individual employees or to
  35|                                 dwelling units in a dwelling, parking spaces for guests or visitors to the use or establishment,
  36|                                 not to exceed 10% of the required parking spaces, shall be located and designated, by signage or
  37|                                 pavement marking, as visitor parking near the principal entrance to the building which they
  38|                                 serve.
  39|                                                                                                    160

===PAGE 161=== (ocr, 38 lines)
   1|                                                                         Annual Town Meeting - May 5, 2016
   2|                                 5. Availability. To ensure the availability and utilization of required parking spaces and loading
   3|                                 bays on a year-round basis:
   4|                                     a. Unless authorized by Special Permit or Site Plan Approval, no fee or other charge to the
   5|                                     parker, in addition to a lease or purchase agreement applicable to occupants generally, shall
   6|                                     be made for a parking space or loading bay required to serve a use, building, or establish-
   7|                                     ment.
   8|                                     b. Each required off-street parking space and loading bay shall be designed so that any motor
   9|                                     vehicle may proceed to and from said space without requiring the moving of any other vehi-
  10|                                     cle or by passing over any other space or bay.
  11|                                     c. Parking spaces for vehicles larger than automobiles, such as large trucks or buses, shall be
  12|                                     specifically identified on the off-street parking and loading plan and shall be of such dimen-
  13|                                     sion as to accommodate the specified type of vehicle. Such vehicles shall be permitted to
  14|                                     park only in the spaces so identified and approved.
  15|                                 6. Snow storage. An aggregate area equal to, at a minimum, ten percent (10%) of the paved area
  16|                                 shall be set aside for snow storage. Snow storage shall be designated, at a minimum, in two sep-
  17|                                 arate and distinct places on the property. These areas shall be located on the submitted off-street
  18|                                 parking and loading plan.
  19|                                In addition snow storage shall be prohibited from:
  20|                                      a. Being located on/in stormwater structures or ponds;
  21|                                      b. Impacting the sight lines at intersections both within the site and where the site driveway
  22|                                      meets public roads.
  23|                                      c. Being located in/on off-street parking spaces that are required per the Protective Bylaw.
  24|                                 7. Surfacing and drainage.
  25|                                     a. All required parking spaces and loading bays, maneuvering aisles, and driveways shall
  26|                                     have a durable, dustless, all-weather surface suitable for year-round use, such as asphalt or
  27|                                     concrete, and shall dispose of surface water by grading and drainage in such a manner that no
  28|                                     surface water shall drain onto any public way or onto any lot in other ownership.
  29|                           b. It is the intent of this Section that the paved surface of a parking lot or loading area shall be lim-
  30|                           ited to such areas as are necessary for the parking spaces, loading bays, maneuvering aisles, and
  31|                           driveways required to meet the provisions of this Section. The off-street parking and loading plan
  32|                           required by this Section shall demonstrate that all paved areas associated with a parking lot are nec-
  33|                           essary for the storing, standing, or maneuvering of vehicles; the permitting authority may deny the
  34|                           request for a permit when more area is paved than is necessary to comply with the provisions of this
  35|                           Section.
  36|                                 8. Grade. The maximum grade of any required maneuvering aisle, parking space, or loading bay
  37|                                 shall be 10%.
  38|                                                                                                    161

===PAGE 162=== (ocr, 37 lines)
   1|                                                                       Annual Town Meeting - May 5, 2016
   2|                                9. Landscaping.
   3|                                     a. On at least three sides of the perimeter of an outdoor parking lot containing 20 or more
   4|                                     parking spaces, there must be at least one tree for every eight parking spaces abutting the
   5|                                     perimeter; such trees must be spaced so that some part of a parking space is not more than
   6|                                     30 feet from the center line of a tree.
   7|                                     b. In the interior part of an outdoor parking lot where two rows of parking spaces containing
   8|                                     a total of 10 or more parking spaces face each other, a landscaped open space not less than
   9|                                     five feet in width must be provided. The landscaped strip may be provided either:
  10|                                          i. Between the rows of parking spaces parallel to the aisle; or
  11|                                          ii. In two or more strips parallel to the spaces and extending from the aisle serving one
  12|                                          row of spaces to the aisle serving the other row of spaces. There must be in each such
  13|                                          strip, at least three trees and in all such strips not fewer than one tree for every eight
  14|                                           parking spaces in the interior part of the parking lot. Trees must be spaced so that some
  15|                                          part of each parking space is not more than 30 feet from a tree.
  16|                                     c. Trees required by this Section shall be at least two inches in diameter at a height four feet
  17|                                     above the ground at time of planting and shall be of a species characterized by suitability
  18|                                     and hardiness for location in a parking lot. To the extent practicable, existing trees shall be
  19|                                    retained and used to satisfy this Section.
  20|                                10. Exception for one-family or two-family dwelling. The provisions of 6.4.3 b) 5; 6.43 e) 4, 5
  21|                                 & 6 with regard to backing into a public street, marking of pavement, moving of vehicles and
  22|                                surfacing and drainage and snow storage shall not apply where parking is provided for any one
  23|                                -family or two-family dwelling.
  24|                                Bicycle Parking
  25|                                1. Required spaces. In any parking area with twenty or more spaces as defined in the parking
  26|                                and loading tables, a minimum of two bicycle parking spaces shall be provided, and one addi-
  27|                                tional bicycle parking space shall be provided for each increment of 20 motor vehicle parking
  28|                                 spaces over 40 vehicle spaces.
  29|                                2. Placement and access. Bicycle parking shall be located near the primary entrance(s) of the
  30|                                building. Half of the bicycle parking spaces shall be provided as long-term parking, safe and
  31|                                secure from vandalism and theft and protected from the elements. The other half shall be pro-
  32|                                vided as short-term (customer or visitor) parking, and short-term parking spaces shall be visi-
  33|                                ble and convenient to the building entrance. Bicycle parking apparatus shall not be installed in
  34|                                a manner that will cause obstruction of pedestrian or motor vehicle traffic. Bicycle parking
  35|                                shall be situated in such a way that normal snow removal activities and snow storage do not
  36|                                impact the bicycle parking facility.
  37|                                                                                                    162

   1|                                                                        Annual Town Meeting - May 5, 2016
   2|                                 3. Dimensional Regulation. Each bicycle parking space shall be sufficient to accommodate a
   3|                                 bicycle six feet in length and two feet in width.
   4|                                 4. Design. Bicycle parking apparatus shall be of a high-security design to which the frame and
   5|                                 wheel of a parked bicycle may be attached; installed in a visible location to deter vandalism
   6|                                 and theft; and permanently mounted to the ground or to a building or other immovable struc-
   7|                                 ture. Inverted-U-frame or other racks that support the bicycle at two or more points above the
   8|                                 center of gravity are required. VOTED 2/3RDS DECLARED to amend the Zoning Bylaw, Sec-

  12|                           ARTICLE 40. To see if the Town will vote to amend the Zoning Bylaw, Section 8.4, Develop-
  13|                           ment Plan Review, by deleting the entirety of the existing wording and inserting a new Section 8.4
  14|                           Site Plan Approval, or take any action relative thereto.
  15|                           (Submitted by Planning Board) Board of Selectmen and Finance Committee will make recommen-
  16|                           dation at town meeting.
  17|                           8.4. Site Plan Approval
  18|                           8.4.1. Purpose
  19|                           The purpose of the Site Plan Review Bylaw hereby established is to protect the safety, public
  20|                           health, convenience and general welfare of the inhabitants of the Town by ensuring that the design
  21|                           and layout of certain developments permitted by right or by Special Permit will constitute suitable
  22|                           developments and will not result in a detriment to the neighborhood or the environment.
  23|                           8.4.2. Applicability
  24|                           8.4.2.1. Projects Requiring Site Plan Review
  25|                                 a) New construction or exterior expansion of any non-residential building or multi-family
  26|                                       dwelling containing more than four (4) units. "Expansion" shall include a floor space in-
  27|                                       crease of 5,000 square feet or 25% or more within any ten (10) year period, whichever is
  28|                                       less.
  29|                                b) The construction or enlargement of any multi-family dwelling containing more than four (4)
  30|                                       units, or buildings accessory to such dwellings, including such dwellings on contiguous
  31|                                      lots under the same ownership.
  32|                                 c) The construction, rehabilitation, of a building involving ten (10) or more parking spaces.
  33|                                 d) The construction, reconfiguration or renovation of parking facilities, with the exception of
  34|                                       normal maintenance.
  35|                                 e) Any use designated that is grated a Special Permit or Variance by the Zoning Board of
  36|                                     Appeals.
  37|                                                                                                    163

===PAGE 164=== (ocr, 37 lines)
   1|                                                                         Annual Town Meeting - May 5, 2016
   2|                               f) Any change of use within an existing building or site.
   3|                               g) Any child care facility.
   4|                         8.4.3. Procedure
   5|                         8.4.3.1. Applications are to be prepared in accordance with the Planning Board Rules and Regulations
   6|                         for Site Plan Approval and Special Permit. These plans are to be submitted to the Planning Office in
   7|                         the number outlined in the aforementioned document.
   8|                         8.4.3.2. Public Hearing on Site Plans
   9|                               a) The Planning Board shall hold a public hearing within sixty-five (65) days of the receipt of a
  10|                                     complete application and shall take final action within ninety (90) days from the time of the
  11|                                     nearıng, as provided for in MGL Chapter 40A, Sections 9 and 11
  12|                               b) The Planning Board's final action, rendered in writing, shall consist of either:
  13|                                 1. Approval of the site plan based upon determination that the proposed plan will constitute a
  14|                                      suitable development and is in compliance with the standards set forth in this Bylaw; or
  15|                                2. Disapproval of the site plan based upon a determination that the proposed plan does not meet
  16|                                      the standards set forth in this Bylaw; or
  17|                                 3. Approval of the site plan subject to any condition, modification or restriction required by the
  18|                                       Planning Board which will ensure that the project meets the standards set forth in this Bylaw.
  19|                         8.4.3.3. Failure of the Board to take final action upon an application for Site Plan Review within the
  20|                         time specified above shall be deemed to be approval of said application. Upon issuance by the Town
  21|                         Clerk of an appropriate certification that the allowed time has passed without Planning Board action,
  22|                         the required building permits may be issued.
  23|                         8.4.4. Site Design Principles
  24|                         8.4.4.1. All Site Plan Review applicants shall adhere to the following general principles when design-
  25|                         ing a site plan for land within the Town of Lunenburg.
  26|                               a) Landscaping Within the Setbacks: Applicants are expected to design setbacks with landscaping,
  27|                                    maintain the landscaping and replace any landscaping that has not fully established itself. Front
  28|                                    yard setback landscaping shall consist of street trees, low-level plantings and other visually
  29|                                    unobtrusive flora. Landscaping shall be situated in a manner such that vehicular and pedestrian
  30|                                    sight lines are not restricted.
  31|                               b) Driveways and Curb Cuts: Entrance and exit location shall be designed to facilitate safe traffic
  32|                                    movement both on and off the site as well as internally. Curb cuts are limited to as few as
  33|                                    practicable for the length of street frontage with adequate separation from adjacent parcels,
  34|                                     other curb cuts and intersections.
  35|                               c) Service Roads/Connection of Parking Lots: To minimize turning movements onto adjacent pub-
  36|                                    lic ways, developers are encouraged to provide internal circulation systems (service roads)
  37|                                                                                                    164

===PAGE 165=== (ocr, 34 lines)
   1|                                                                         Annual Town Meeting - May 5, 2016
   2|                                      that connect to adjacent developments (parking area to parking area. Site plans that pro-
   3|                                       pose service roads and/or connection of parking areas shall show on the plan how the con-
   4|                                       nection of parking areas will be achieved and have a deeded agreement and easement from
   5|                                       the abutter.
   6|                                 d) Parking and Loading Areas:
   7|                                 1. Access locations shall be designed to encourage unimpeded traffic flow with controlled
   8|                                       turning movements and minimum hazards to vehicular and pedestrian traffic.
   9|                                 2. Facilities and access routes for deliveries, service and maintenance shall be separated, where
  10|                                       practical, from public access routes and parking areas.
  11|                                 3. Parking facilities shall be designed to prevent parked cars from damaging trees, shrubs and
  12|                                       curbing, and shall not disrupt pedestrian walkways.
  13|                                 4. Walkways shall be separated from areas of vehicular traffic and designed to minimize
  14|                                       stormwater runoff while maximizing access.
  15|                                 5. These facilities shall include appropriate landscaping, pedestrian access and multi-modal
  16|                                       parking accommodations.
  17|                                 e) Stormwater Management (Grading and Drainage): All site plans shall be designed to com-
  18|                                       ply with DEP (Department of Environmental Protection) Stormwater Management Stand-
  19|                                       ards. Furthermore, the use of Low Impact Development, infiltration of stormwater and re-
  20|                                       duction of impervious surface are strongly encouraged.
  21|                                 f) Lighting: Lighting designs shall be Dark Sky compliant. This includes all exterior lights
  22|                                       being designed and installed in such a manner as to prevent objectionable light at (and
  23|                                       glare across) the property lines. Developments shall eliminate glare onto adjacent proper-
  24|                                       ties through the use of lighting shields, earthen berms, addition of new landscaping and/or
  25|                                       retention of existing natural vegetation.
  26|                            8.4.5. Standards for Site Plan Review
  27|                            8.4.5.1. In reviewing a site plan application, the Planning Board shall take the following items into
  28|                            consideration:
  29|                                 a) Compliance with all applicable provisions of the Lunenburg Protective Bylaw; Planning
  30|                                       Board Rules and Regulations for Site Plan Approval and Special Permit; and Subdivision
  31|                                       Regulations.
  32|                                 b) Traffic safety and ease of access at street and highway entrances and exits, to be determined
  33|                                       by
  34|                                                                                                    165

===PAGE 166=== (ocr, 30 lines)
   1|                                                                         Annual Town Meeting - May 5, 2016
   2|                               1. The adequacy of grades,
   3|                                2. The presence of sight distances that are appropriate for the design speed of the road, as
   4|                                    determined by ASHTO,
   5|                                3. Minimum safe distance between such exits and entrances, and
   6|                                4. The existence of proper alignment or safe distance from proximal existing streets) and drive
   7|                                     way entrances.
   8|                               c) Safety and adequacy of driveway layout and pedestrian walkways; off-street parking areas; off-
   9|                                     street loading areas for materials and products; to be determined by
  10|                          1. Adequate access for service and emergency vehicles,
  11|                         2. Proper separation of pedestrian and vehicular facilities,
  12|                          3. The presence of separated facilities for delivery of goods, and
  13|                         4. Acceptable traffic patterns to/from the site entrance/exit and within the parking facilities.
  14|                               d) Safe and adequate means of disposal of sewage, garbage and rubbish.
  15|                               e) Safe and adequate water supply and distribution; including sufficient water and facilities for
  16|                                     firefighting on the site.
  17|                                 f) Safe and adequate storm drainage consistent with
  18|                                  1. building and surface coverage,
  19|                                    2. grade and slopes,
  20|                                   3. soils and
  21|                                   4. water table
  22|                          All of which shall result in zero increase in the rate of runoff from the site, as measured by the 2-Year
  23|                         (24-hour) and 10-Year (24-hour) Storm Event Standards; and there will be no negative impacts to
  24|                         downstream property-owners in a 100-year (24-hour) storm event. The stormwater system shall meet
  25|                         all the requirements and standards of the Massachusetts Stormwater Management Standards, as
  26|                          amended.
  27|                               g) Prevention of soil erosion during and after construction; provisions for an increase in the vol-
  28|                                     ume of runoff of surface water from the site and the protection of adjacent areas against detri-
  29|                                     mental or offensive uses on the site by the provision of adequate buffers against light, sight,
  30|                                                                                                    166

===PAGE 167=== (ocr, 37 lines)
   1|                                                                        Annual Town Meeting - May 5, 2016
   2|                                sound, dust and vibrations.
   3|                                   h) Open space provisions and landscaping, including the maximum retention of on-site natu-
   4|                                      ral features.
   5|                                   i) Placement of underground utilities, night lighting and signs.
   6|                                   j) Compatibility of soil and subsoil's to type and intensity of development.
   7|                                   k) No exterior lighting shall shine directly on adjacent properties, or in such a manner as to
   8|                                      create a nuisance on such properties or a hazard on public ways. To ensure exterior lighting
   9|                                      conforms to this provision the following conditions shall be met.
  10|                                     1. For the proposed use all lighting installed shall conform to the standards outlined in The

  23|                                      in Chapter 131 section 40 Massachusetts General Laws, and the Lunenburg Wetlands By-
  24|                                      law, as amended
  25|                           8.4.6. Modifications to an Approved Site Plan
  26|                           8.4.6.1. Once a site plan has been approved by the Planning Board, said plan shall not be changed,
  27|                           amended or modified without approval of the Board. There shall be only one final site plan in ef-
  28|                           fect for a parcel of land at a time.
  29|                           8.4.7. Construction of an Approved Site Plan
  30|                           8.4.7.1. Construction on a site with an approved site plan must be started within one (1) year from
  31|                           the date of the Planning Board's approval of the site plan. Site plan approval may be extended for
  32|                           one (1) year at the discretion of the Board after receipt of a written request from the landowner,
  33|                           showing good cause. If one year has elapsed from the date of approval; and no extensions have
  34|                           been granted; or if one year has elapsed since the granting of said extension; the final site plan ap-
  35|                           proval shall become null and void without requiring any further action by the Planning Board.
  36|                           This time period shall not include delays resulting from litigation.
  37|                                                                                                    167

   1|                                                                       Annual Town Meeting - May 5, 2016
   2|                          8.4.7.2. No permit to build, alter or expand any building or structure, or change of use requiring Site
   3|                          Plan Review under this Bylaw shall be issued by the Building Inspector; nor shall any construction
   4|                          commence before a written statement of Approval or Approval with Conditions has been issued by
   5|                          the Planning Board.
   6|                          8.4.7.3. The Building Inspector may inspect a site under construction for compliance with the ap-
   7|                          proved site plan.
   8|                          8.4.8. Enforcement
   9|                          8.4.8.1. It shall be the duty of the Building Inspector/Zoning Enforcement Officer to administer and
  10|                          enforce the provisions of this Bylaw.
  11|                          8.4.8.2. The Planning Board shall provide a schedule of inspection fees to be paid by the applicant
  12|                          prior to the start of construction.
  13|                          8.4.8.3. The Planning Board may use inspection fees paid by the applicant to offset the cost of hir-
  14|                          ing any additional engineers or inspectors necessary to monitor all phases of construction covered
  15|                          by an approved site plan.
  16|                          8.4.8.4. If work is not performed per the approved plan, including all conditions, the Building In-
  17|                          spector/Zoning Enforcement Officer has the authority to issue a cease and desist order per the Plan-
  18|                          ning Board Rules and Regulations for Site Plan Approval and Special Permits, as amended.
  19|                          8.4.8.5. "As Built" plans, certified by a registered professional and noting any change from the ap-
  20|                          proved plan, shall be filed with the Building Inspector and the Planning Board before a Certificate
  21|                         of Completion shall be issued.
  22|                          8.4.8.6. Building Inspector shall issue a Certificate of Completion when all construction has been
  23|                          performed and all other requirements have been met in compliance with the approved site plan. A
  24|                          copy of the Certificate of Completion will be filed with the Planning Board.
  25|                          8.4.8.7. No Certificate of Occupancy shall be issued for any structure or use subject to site plan re-
  26|                         view unless a Certificate of Completion has been issued.
  27|                          8.4.8.8. The Building Inspector may issue a Temporary Certificate of Occupancy for a period of one
  28|                         (1) year if the required construction has been substantially completed and the permitted uses of the
  29|                          development can be carried on in a safe and convenient manner.
  30|                          8.4.9. Rules and Regulations

  32|                          this Section. The Board may, where such action allowed by law, in the public interest and not in-
  33|                          consistent with the purpose and intent of this Bylaw, waive strict compliance with any requirement
  34|                          of this Site Plan Review Bylaw or its rules and regulations.

   6|                            CHANGES new language SHOWN IN strikethrough
   7|                            SECTION 2: JURISDICTION ~ Except as permitted by the Conservation Commission or as pro-
   8|                            vided in this bylaw, no person shall commence to remove, fill, dredge, build upon, degrade, dis-
   9|                            charge into, or otherwise alter the following resource areas: any freshwater wetlands; marshes;
  10|                            wet meadows; bogs; swamps; vernal pools; banks; reservoirs; lakes; ponds of any size; creeks;
  11|                            beaches; lands under water bodies; lands subject to flooding or inundation by groundwater or sur-
  12|                            face water; lands subject to flooding; lands within 100 feet of any of the aforesaid resource areas;
  13|                            perennial rivers and streams and lands within 200 feet of such rivers and streams (collectively the
  14|                            "resource areas protected by this bylaw"). Said resource areas shall be protected whether or not
  15|                            they border surface waters. (ADD THE FOLLOWING SENTENCE TO THIS SECTION) Lands
  16|                            within 100 feet of aforesaid resource areas and within 200 feet of rivers or perennial streams lo-
  17|                            cated within the corporate boundary of Lunenburg shall be protected regardless of the location of
  18|                            the wetland with respect to municipal corporate boundary.
  19|                            SECTION 5: NOTICE OF HEARINGS ~ Any person filing a permit application or (REMOVE
  20|                            RFD AND ADD) RDA with the Commission at the same time shall give written notice thereof, by
  21|                            certified mail (return receipt requested), (ADD) certificate of mail or hand delivery, to all abutters
  22|                            according to the most recent applicable tax list of the assessors, including owners of land directly
  23|                            opposite on any public or private street or way, including any in another municipality or across a
  24|                            body of water. The notice to abutters shall enclose a copy of the permit application or request,
  25|                            with plans, or shall state where copies may be examined and obtained by abutters. An affidavit of
  26|                            the person providing such notice, with a copy of the notice mailed or delivered, shall be filed with
  27|                            the Commission. When a person requesting a determination is other than the owner, the request,
  28|                            the notice of the hearing and the determination itself shall be sent by the Commission to the owner
  29|                            as well as to the person making the request.
  30|                            The Commission shall conduct a public hearing on any application or (REMOVE RFD AND
  31|                            ADD) RDA, with written notice given at the expense of the applicant, five business days prior to
  32|                            the hearing, in a newspaper of general circulation in the municipality.
  33|                            The Commission shall commence the public hearing within 21 days from receipt of a completed
  34|                            application or request for determination unless an extension is authorized in writing by the appli-
  35|                            cant.
  36|                            The Commission shall issue its permit or determination in writing within 21 days of the close of
  37|                            the public hearing thereon unless an extension is authorized in writing by the applicant.
  38|                            The Commission in an appropriate case may combine its hearing under this bylaw with the hear-
  39|                            ing conducted under the Wetlands Protection Act, G.L. Ch.131, s. 40, and Regulations, 310 CMR
  40|                            10.00.
  41|                                                                                                    171

===PAGE 172=== (ocr, 42 lines)
   1|                                                                        Annual Town Meeting - May 5, 2016
   2|                           The Commission shall have authority to continue the hearing, (ADD) with the applicant's consent
   3|                           written or granted verbally on the record during the course of the public hearing or meeting, to a
   4|                           certain date announced at the hearing, for reasons stated at the hearing, which may include receipt
   5|                           of additional information from the applicant or others deemed necessary by the Commission in its
   6|                           discretion, or comments and recommendations of boards and officials listed in Section 6.
   7|                           SECTION 6: COORDINATION WITH OTHER BOARDS ~ Any person filing a Wetlands Protec-
   8|                           tion Act Permit application or (REMOVE RFD AND ADD) RDA with the Commission shall pro-
   9|                           vide written notice thereof at the same time, by certified mail or hand delivery, to the Board of"
  10|                           Selectmen, Planning Board, Zoning Board of Appeals, Board of Health and Building Inspector.
  11|                           The Commission shall not take final action until such boards and officials have had fourteen (14)
  12|                           days from receipt of notice to file written comments and recommendations with the Commission,
  13|                           which the Commission shall take into account, but which shall not be binding on the Commission.
  14|                           The applicant shall have the right to receive any such comments and recommendations, and to re-
  15|                           spond to them at a hearing of the Commission, prior to final action.
  16|                           SECTION 7: PERMITS AND CONDITIONS
  17|                           (CHANGE TO PARAGRAPH 6)
  18|                           A permit shall expire three years from the date of issuance. Notwithstanding the above, the Com-
  19|                           mission in its discretion may issue a permit expiring five years from the date of issuance for recur-
  20|                           ring or continuous maintenance work, provided that annual notification of time and location of
  21|                           work is given to the Commission. (ADD) The Commission may extend a permit or a previous ex-
  22|                           tension for an additional period of no greater than three years per extension. Any permit may be
  23|                           renewed once for an additional one year period, provided that a request for a renewal is received in
  24|                           writing by the Commission prior to expiration. Notwithstanding the above, a permit may contain
  25|                           requirements which shall be enforceable for a stated number of years, indefinitely, or until perma-
  26|                           nent protection is in place, and shall apply to all owners of the land.
  27|                           SECTION 8. REGULATIONS~ After public notice and public hearing, the Commission shall
  28|                           promulgate rules and regulations to effectuate the purposes of this bylaw. Failure by the Commis-
  29|                           sion to promulgate such rules and regulations or a legal declaration of their invalidity by a court of
  30|                           law shall not3 act to suspend or invalidate the effect of this bylaw.
  31|                           At a minimum these regulations shall define key terms in this bylaw not inconsistent with the by-
  32|                           law and procedures governing the amount and filing of fees. (ADD THE FOLLOWING PARA-
  33|                           GRAPH) The Conservation Commission will establish a draft of proposed changes during regular,
  34|                           properly posted open meetings. Once there is consensus on the proposed changes, a formal hear-
  35|                           ing will be scheduled and will take place over 2 regularly scheduled meetings. Both hearings will
  36|                           be televised. A notice of the hearings will be put in a local newspaper at least 10 business days
  37|                           prior to the hearing dates. At the discretion of the Conservation Commission, the hearing may be
  38|                           continued to the next consecutive open, regularly scheduled meeting. Once the hearing is closed,
  39|                           the final changes will be drafted and voted on. The proposed and approved changes will be posted
  40|                           on the Commission's Town website. VOTED UNANIMOUSLY to amend the General Bylaws of
  41|                           the Town, Section XXI, General Wetlands Bylaw, as printed in the warrant.
